Summary

"It's a game, Agatha. Don't play it."

Agatha is an FBI agent tasked with finding an elusive and psychopathic serial killer they've nicknamed "The Green Witch".

Suddenly finding herself at the center of this killer's attention, Agatha's already dubious moral compass spins out of control.

A tale of mutual obsession based on Killing Eve and Criminal Minds.

    Chapter 1: Down The Road

    Chapter Notes Wrote this while wine drunk. English is not my first language, it will be horny soon. Enjoy.

    She was late.

    Agatha Harkness had a strict morning routine, but yesterday was just one of those nights. One of the cases she had been working on for months was really getting to her. She had found herself drinking a bit too much wine in front of the files at home, staring at the crime scene photos with anger. The answer was there, the files had everything she needed to figure it all out - and people’s lives depended on it - but her brain just wouldn’t focus. It was like this every November. The anniversary of Nicky’s death always took a toll on her. No matter how long ago it was, no matter what she had going on, every November her brain shut down and all she could think of was her late son, 6 years old - one day laughing while hiking with her in the woods, the other day gone in the face of a doctor telling her they did “everything they could”.

    Granted, her job wasn’t easy either, and she would often get burnt out and distracted like this. But in any other month Agatha would just shake it off. She had worked too hard and for too long to let tiredness get on her way. Her position in the FBI was one of authority. She was unit chief, respected by her colleagues - or at least feared by them - and that required discipline, it required control.

    In the Behavior Analysis Unit, Agatha and her coworkers spent their time hunting down the most complex criminal minds of the country. They analyzed the crime, and, from it, they built a profile - the type of person who would commit it - and narrowed the suspect pool, sometimes slightly, sometimes down to one possible name. Just like that, they made sure serial killers, mass murderers, terrorists and the like were caught and put behind bars before they could hurt anyone else.

    The nobleness of the job wasn’t interesting to Agatha, as harsh as that sounds. After Nicky, she couldn’t give two shits about who else lived or died. What interested her was the hunt. More often than not, Agatha hunted people who thought of themselves as gods - too good to be ever caught by the cops, too smart to be ever figured out by those around them. Untouchable. Tsk. Agatha knew better. The cops might be useless, their loved ones might be gullible, but Agatha was just as much a god as they were. There was nothing they could do to escape her. Agatha would win at the end.

    She didn’t share that view with the team, of course. Agatha did her best to seem like one of them - caring and compassionate.

    She considered herself a cold ruthless woman, yet secretly, she did have a soft spot for her coworkers. They weren't morons. Huge compliment, considering the rest of the people in the FBI building. Billy was so young, he constantly reminded her of Nicky - of how Nicky would be when he grew up. Could've been . Brilliant, caring, always seeing the good in people, but knowing enough about the world to not be too naive. The boy really liked Agatha, for reasons the unit chief couldn’t really understand. Maybe it was the gay thing, solidarity or whatever. Maybe it was her position in the bureau - the chief - making her seem like a professional ambitious career-driven woman. Admirable, almost. Someone to learn from. He always seemed to defend her against Jen or Alice - also part of the team - when Agatha was being a bit too much of an ass for their liking. It happened often. They weren't morons, but they dabbled.

    Alone in her apartment, Agatha checked the clock. 2AM. A sigh escaped her lips. She looked at the empty wine bottle and decided to chug the rest of her glass, closing one of the files scattered across her office table and knowing she would hate herself in the morning for staying up so late.

    Yet, when the harsh sound of her alarm clock ripped her out of a deep sleep, she didn’t really hate herself. She understood. It was only the first week of November. She'd probably do way worse by the end of the month.

    With another sigh, Agatha started her morning routine. Every day at 6AM she would drive over to the nearest park and jog through the woods. It brought her comfort to go down that road paved with dirt - the same one she used to bring Nicky to - and it got her blood running for the day. She usually started getting ready without issue, never one to stay lazing in bed. Today, however, she allowed herself to hit snooze. The sunlight on her eyes was a bit much. So yeah, she was late.

    Agatha managed to get out of bed at 7AM, knowing her jog would have to be shorter than usual if she wanted to get back and shower before work at 8. Grabbing the first thing that sort of resembled workout clothes - a tank top and booty shorts - she put on her socks and running shoes before going down to the kitchen.

    Her phone was, of course, bustling with notifications. Being unit chief also meant dealing with a lot of bureaucratic bullshit Agatha honestly couldn’t care less about. Starting the coffee machine and filling up the toaster with bread, the woman sighed as she assumed the professional character she had so carefully curated, carefully answering the e-mails and slack messages those suit-and-tie idiots considered urgent. One notification was actually useful. New body found - the same serial killer case she was going over yesterday. Agatha sighed. Fuck.

    Opening the email, she carefully went over the information. New York - oh, this one was close. She knew the killer was active all over, so the fact that this happened so close to Quantico, Virginia, got her excited. She was all about the hunt, after all. Victim number 9. Male, as always. Found in bed, as always. Fully castrated, as always. A leaf-looking symbol carved into their chest with a curved knife… as always.

    Complex signature. Agatha’s team had been over this. They had been studying this killer ever since Victim number 5. This unknown subject - unsub - fit the profile of what was called a “black widow” killer in her field of work: female, killing sexual partners for money or power. Intelligent, manipulative, and highly organized in their kills. They usually go through a long cycle of meeting and manipulating their victims until killing them off and running away with their money.

    This one, however, was different. Her cooling period was shorter, she didn’t bother developing relationships with the powerful men she killed. She didn’t want inheritance money or pensions: she wanted a night with them. A luxurious expensive date. All the victims had been last seen with a woman most commonly described by witnesses with the words “beautiful” and “quiet”.

    She drove Agatha mad.

    The nerve of this woman! Going out in public every time, and yet never giving them more than two grainy CCTV clips of the back of her head - once blonde, once ginger. Most likely wearing wigs and other disguises. She seemed to be a goddamn ghost of sorts. They had interviewed countless people regarding the 8 previous murders connected to this unsub, but nothing came of it. With the victims having no personal relation to their killer, it was nearly impossible to catch her if she didn’t screw up forensically…

    and she never, ever, makes a mistake.

    Yes, Agatha had a penchant for female killers. She couldn’t deny it. Male ones were boring: impotent, mommy never gave you love, women never looked at you twice, boo-hoo-hoo. Women wanted power, and Agatha could relate to that. It was her field of work, after all. What she's known for. Agatha had countless books on the killer female psyche. That was her area of expertise in the team, and it was the work that got her to unit chief. She checked the victim’s profile. He was a big-shot CEO. Republican, involved with Crypto, cheating on his wife. Classic. Clicking on his picture, she was even less surprised: balding, fat, greasy. Yawn. Agatha couldn’t help but wonder if the killer found him as repulsive - or at least as boring - as she did. If so, why would she ever subject herself to a date with men like these. Killing them for millions in inheritance was one thing, but for a fancy dinner? To be paraded as a trophy wife around Michelin star restaurants? It didn’t make sense. The Green Witch is too good for this.

    She shook the thought away, trying to remain objective.

    They advocated against giving unsubs nicknames. It was generally a bad idea, it inflated their ego, straightened their resolve and made them more likely to take their actions up a notch. “Zodiac”, “Vampire of Sacramento”, “Night Stalker”... Agatha wondered why they had to sound so badass. If it were up to her, they’d name serial killers something pathetic like “Impotent Pete” or “Tiny Dick Greg”. Maybe that would make it less appealing. Yet, within the FBI headquarters, with the leaf-symbol and the seemingly magical way she never left forensic evidence, they started calling her “The Green Witch” regardless. Jen said it first, and Agatha reprimanded her, of course. It was Agatha’s favorite pasttime activity at work - bickering with her infuriating subordinate. After the third month of investigation, and of having the file on standby - because it seemed fucking unsolvable - Agatha gave in and started using the nickname with the rest of the team. It was better than “the leaf-carving- unsub”, or whatever bullshit Agatha made up in order to avoid saying Green Witch. She sighed and locked her phone. Work wasn’t until eight. This Green Bitch could wait for her to have some buttered toast.

    Breakfast made her feel warmer, but yesterday’s wine was still present in the shape of a nagging headache. When taking the driver’s seat of her SUV, she made sure to open the window to alleviate any hangover sickness. With that, Agatha distractedly drove out of her garage, humming a song.

    She didn’t notice at first, but it was Nicky’s song she was humming - the ballad. A song they crafted together, about walking down the park roads. It was her son who first sang it - Agatha didn’t know where he got the melody from, if from anywhere - but the sight of her little boy being so happy and content while skipping through the woods, hand in hers, was her most treasured memory. “Down, down, down the road, down the wind-y road…” he sang once, out of the blue, as carefree as a child should be. If she closed her eyes and focused, she could still hear his voice. The song comforted her. It made her miss him just a little bit less. It made her feel like he was still aliv-

    “SHIT,” Agatha hit the brakes. “WHAT THE FUCK?” she yelled at the pedestrian who was wide-eyed staring at the front of her car.

    With a sigh, Agatha closed her eyes and hardened her grip around the steering wheel. Holy shit, she had almost killed that woman. Her thoughts immediately turned defensive: she was distracted, sure, but it wasn’t her fault! The girl was fucking jaywalking. “WHAT THE FUCK ARE YOU DOING?” Agatha screamed through the open window, quickly unbuckling and getting out of the car. The woman stayed silent, surprisingly calm. She just looked at Agatha in what could be mistaken as shock to anyone else. Agatha knows shock, and this wasn’t it. There was a hint of a smirk on the girl’s face… she thought Agatha’s desperation was funny.

    “I ALMOST HIT YOU!” She tried to get a real reaction out of the stranger, explaining the obvious so that whoever this girl was would react appropriately. No success. “Yeah, no shit.” The girl answers, matter-of-factly, no sense of danger in her voice. The brunette had big brown eyes, her skin darker than Agatha’s. Her hair was tied up in a ponytail and she was casually taking her airpods off. The woman was wearing casual clothes - black boots, black jeans, and a dark green crop top that seemed to have a floral pattern. She was fit. Agatha took her in while trying to calm down. Fuck, that was a close call. “You good?” the girl asked with a smile.

    “Me?” a scoff. “Yeah… I’m good. Are you?”

    “I’m great.” - and… oh… the stranger was looking Agatha up and down, suddenly making the agent very self conscious about her own attire. They lock eyes.

    “Well… try not to jaywalk next time, asshole.”

    “Try not to run me over next time, sweetheart.”

    Agatha laughed with another scoff. The nerve of this woman! Shaking her head and getting back into her car, she made sure to slam the door. To her surprise, when looking back up she found the girl still standing in the middle of the road, watching her. They locked eyes through the windshield glass.

    “Are you gonna fucking move or what?”

    The stranger slowly crossed the road, never breaking eye contact. Once she hit the sidewalk, she smiled.

    “Te veo,” she says, and walks away, down the road from which Agatha came. That was Spanish, she noticed. “I’ll see you”...

    What the fuck? Agatha took a deep breath and shook her head as if to forcibly throw the woman out of her thoughts. What a little shit.

    She hit the gas with a little bit too much force and went on her way.

    Chapter 2: A door appears

    Chapter Summary The team goes over The Green Witch's latest victim. They make a discovery that shouldn't excite Agatha quite like it does.

    Back in the bureau after her morning run and shower, Agatha was feeling better. She had put on her favorite outfit - always a delight when all the components were clean and ready to wear - and made sure she looked perfect before leaving the house. A white blouse, half open, purple pants, heels, and her favorite dark blue coat. Perfect. She had Nicky’s locket on, of course, she always did. This outfit matched it perfectly. Maybe that’s why she liked it so much.

    Entering the bustling FBI building, Agatha quickly slid into an empty elevator, clicking the ‘close door’ button multiple times. For a second there, she thought she would be able to enjoy a few extra minutes of peace and solitude. Silly optimism.

    Someone’s hand popped in at the last minute (because of course it did) stopping the elevator doors with a loud metallic noise. Agatha sighed. Two people walked into the tiny space. At least they were people she had authority over, Agatha thought.

    Alice was wearing her signature leather jacket, Lilia, wore… Jesus Christ - Agatha winced - the most colorful silk cardigan she had ever seen. Whoever designed that should be arrested. “Good morning, chief,” the youngest of the three greeted her nonchalantly. Agatha, too preoccupied with pulling her phone out of her pocket, only replied with a grunt. Alice Wu-Gulliver was a kind person. Smart and sympathetic, she had whatever the opposite of Resting Bitch Face was. The red streaks of hair and punk rock outfits seemed almost out of place on her, if you judged only by looks. “A lot of emotional baggage” was most people’s first thought when hearing the name Alice Wu-Gulliver. The same way people feel when hearing the name of a child star like “Macaulay Culkin”. But Alice hadn't been a child actor. She wasn’t the star, her mom was. Lorna Wu-Gulliver’s songs played non-stop on the radio back in the day. Agatha still remembered trying to hate it just because it was mainstream, but secretly listening to it while doing chores. After Lorna’s tragic death, her only daughter had “spiraled” - or at least that was what the media had reported. Constantly reported. The general public was well aware of Alice’s troubled years, her drug abuse, her run-ins with the law… not a lot of people knew she had turned her life around and joined the police force, later making it into the FBI. Everyone knew about Alice’s past, and Agatha knew it couldn’t be easy. She herself had gone through many lengths to ensure no one ever learned about Nicky.

    Today, Alice was the undisputed best agent they had when it came to forensic speech science. As soon as someone opened their mouth in the interrogation room, the woman would closely examine every word they uttered, reading the truth behind them as easily as one would read a children's book. It was impressive. Written or verbal communication with the cops was usually full of protective walls, of things unsaid or ornamental language designed to hide the truth. Nothing went past Alice.

One of the oldest agents still active, Lilia Calderu had earned quite an unique reputation within the FBI. Agents all around would constantly call her for consults, believing she had some sort of magic psychic ability. Agatha knew it was bullshit, of course, Calderu’s knowledge came from her years of experience, but it was still amusing to see how widespread the belief of Lilia’s clairvoyance was - especially between seemingly rational and down-to-earth law enforcement professionals.

    She wore her curly gray hair in the same style as always - a messy up do. Agatha could never figure out how exactly her hair stayed in place, she saw no hair ties or bobby pins. Lilia dressed like anyone you would expect to encounter in a “magical items” antique store. Draped in silk and always wearing linen pants, she always looked like she was about to sell you some bullshit trinkets and crystals, swearing by their otherworldly properties. Agatha hated her the least. The woman had so much experience, the team often treated her gut feelings as facts. She was scarily accurate.

Pity was the last thing she wanted to see in anyone’s eyes when looking at her. “No, but The Green Witch got herself a new boyfriend.”

It's hard to connect cases in which the victim doesn’t know the assailant, and even harder when the bodies are distributed all over the country. The homicide departments of each state don't really share case files with one another. So was Agatha.

They all looked at the picture Calderu had displayed in the middle of the table. “There, that symbol.” She pointed, and the team had to squint to know what she was talking about. “It… looks like a heart!” Billy exclaimed.

Sure enough, next to the bedside post was a perfectly drawn black heart, about the size of a quarter. How did Lilia even notice it? The thought weakly echoed on the back of her brain.     That’s romantic.

    The voice that immediately understood what the heart meant.

    Agatha Harkness was the best at catching female serial killers for a reason she refused to ever acknowledge as truth, yet got constantly reminded of by her own brain: she was just like them. She intimately understood their drive to seek power, the ruthlessness of their ways, the coldness of their actions. Most of them found power in a big pile of cash, others, however, were a bit more complex. Agatha was one of those women. Complex.

She was their main specialist for this specific case - and all involving female criminals - but, in moments like these, Agatha had to carefully organize her mind before opening her mouth. Rare moments, sure, but pivotal ones. She just shook her head no, and the team knew it meant “not now” .

    You know who that was for, don’t you?

    It meant the heart is a real lead.

    “The NYPD cops thought it was part of the.. uh… fetish thing. They didn’t think it was important!”

    “That’s so stupid,” Jen said Agatha’s thoughts out loud.

    “They’re going back to take better pictures of it,” Hart continued, “but the lead investigator said it’s safe to assume it was left by the killer.”

    “Why’s that?” Agatha spit out quickly. She didn’t trust the lead investigator to make any assumptions. Or anyone else, for that matter.

    “They examined the victim’s computer and found many videos of his… bedroom activities. The last one...” Hart checked her phone. “Was recorded just a couple of days before he died. Nothing on the wall.”

    Alice leaned in to examine the picture closer, seeming a bit more confused than she did a second ago.

This happened frequently: they took a case and worked it unofficially until the bureaucratic nightmare of dealing with so many state laws was over. After victim eight, the case was publicly under FBI investigation, and only one team was equipped to deal with it. The BAU. Agatha's team.

    She knows you’re on the team. Female killers. It’s your specialty.

    “It’s settled, then. Let’s get to work.”

Turned out to be easy: Agatha liked her more than she liked the FBI. delightful.

It would probably piss Agatha off, but at this point, it wouldn’t break them up.

Agatha, studying Rio... furrowing her brows, long thick hair up in a bun, clenching her very defined jawline. Oooh, maybe she’d be wearing those big ass glasses of hers.

I was on vacation.” “Ah,” Natahsa smiled, nodding as if she was just now remembering the serial killer frenzy Rio had unleashed this year - a nation-wide panic, courtesy of leaf carvings and chopped off penises. “Of course. She didn’t go there looking for a way out of Konstantin’s ‘business’, she wanted to free Agatha.

She looked at the heavy door that locked them both in - with its stupid little window, and a stupid little guard on the other side, pretending not to look.     Rio Vidal never flinched.

    Except-

Rio knew Agatha well by this point, had been studying the agent for months - just like Agatha, knowingly or now, had been studying her too. Rio knew Agatha just wanted to see if she could scare her. She knew Rio was able to feel fear, of course, she wanted to know if she could do it.

    They had lied to her. Her love for Agatha proved them wrong. She wasn’t just born to kill - her life could be more than that.

    Still, when her shiv darted toward Natasha, Rio aimed for the neck.

    Chapter 24: Which side are you on?

    You’re dead meat?

    Which side are you on?

    It did. It made sense at the time. The Green Witch had castrated and carved those nine men because it meant something to her, otherwise, she wouldn’t have taken the risk. The signature was the only thing connecting otherwise completely isolated murders. Which organization in their right mind would let a hitman make it so easy on the cops?

    Plus, it answered a question that had been bothering Agatha from the start - why did The Green Witch do it all for one fancy date night? She was too good for that. With her talent, she could easily lure men in, strip them of their fortunes, kill them however she wanted and vanish into thin air - then do it again when the money ran out. So why didn’t she? Doing it for hire explained it - she didn’t need the money, she didn’t need the dates. She was just having fun. Work and play. The unsub was getting more fascinating by the second. Maybe she fucking called it in.

    Agatha could laugh. Because, yeah, maybe Rio was trying to impress - the same way someone “casually mentions” a fancy job in a bar full of pretty girls. Agatha would know, she’s done it. A badge can go a long fucking way.

    The BAU had quickly painted a picture that seemed just perfect: one of a lone black widow assassin, picking up cases as vigilantism. Agatha couldn’t help but believe that the profile still held some truth to it. But it was unequivocally wrong. It missed the most important part of the puzzle - the part she now knew, but couldn’t tell anyone about, lest she’d put a target in their fucking backs.

    The Green Witch wasn’t a lone assassin. She had gone rogue. Taken risks she shouldn't have, because they meant something to her. The signature was a risk. Agatha was another. Fucking idiot. She’s in over her head.

    The reality of the situation settled again around her. She glanced at the office couch and at the improvised bed she had set up there. Then, Agatha turned to the landline installed on top of her desk. It was for inner communications only - within the building - but that was the last thing Agatha cared about right now. She dialed the combination that let her make outside phone calls, and went right back to Wanda. This time, unmonitored. Except for the whole FBI force, of course. But who gave a shit?

    Beep-

    “Agatha, what the fuck was that?” Wanda sounded furious.

    Gaslight.

    “I had the TV on in the background.”

    “No you didn’t.”

    “Yes I did.”

    “There’s not even a fucking TV in the office.”

    “I added one.”

    “And what are you doing in the damn office anyway?”

    Finally, out of the woods. “Research!”

    “At one in the goddamn morning?!”

    “Yes, and I need your help.”

    “Who bugged your phone?”

    Agatha ignored it. “Do you have your work laptop with you?”

    “What-” Wanda sighed, exasperated. “It’s one in the goddamn morning!” Irrelevant.

    Agatha rolled her eyes. “And is your laptop there?”

    “No, Agatha. It’s downstairs in the living room. I’m UPstairs because I was in bed. With my husband. Cause it’s one-”

    “In the goddamn morning, yeah, yeah. Here’s the thing...” She dragged a hand down her face and took a deep breath. “There’s a bug in ViCap. An error. It might compromise the entire system. I need your help.”

    There was a slight pause. Agatha could almost see Wanda’s concerned, professional face appear.

    “What do you mean?”

    “It won’t let me access certain files. People.”

    “Do you have a lead?”

    Agatha paused. “...One could say that.”

    “Is that who bugged your phone? I told you to encrypt it. Since when-” Clever little shit.

    “Wanda! No one bugged my phone,” she lied. “Can you help me or not?”

    Wanda sighed. “What’s the error message?”

    “Protected File. You do not have the clearance level to access this information. Please contact-”

    “It’s not a bug. You don’t have the clearance.”

    “I can see fuckers in witness protection! Who are these people?”

    “I don’t know! You tell me!”

    “With this level of encryption, only from the office. I’ll try tomorrow, but no promises.” “Fuck,” Agatha cussed to herself.

    “Agatha.” Wanda’s tone was somber. “You never gave a shit about the system. What’s going on?”

    She didn’t answer.

    “Why are you still in the office? Why didn’t you contact IT?”

    For once, Agatha had no lies to tell.

    “You can’t, can you? Agatha what are you doing?”

    She sighed. “Sorry for the late call. It’s fine.”

    “Agnes.”

    Ouch.

    “Thank you for picking up, toots. Really. Go back to sleep. I’ll handle it.” She hung up the phone again. Agatha had no fucking idea how to handle it. “Absolutely not.”

    Agatha barely had time to turn around before a hand ripped the cigarette from between her lips. Wanda stomped on it with her ridiculous knee-high scarlet boots. “What the fuck, Maximoff?”

    She thought the other side of the parking lot would’ve been a hidden enough spot. After last night’s call fiasco with Wanda, she really didn’t want the redhead - or the rest of the BAU - to see her stress smoking. The last thing Agatha needed right now was a lecture on doing things she knew damn well were bad for her - but judging by Wanda’s face, that’s exactly what she was about to get.

    “I could ask you the same thing, Agatha. What the fuck? You’re back to smoking?” Agatha. Not Agnes. She was already in trouble.

    “Nope. We need to talk first.”

    Agatha groaned. With an eye roll, she reached into her pocket, pulling out her pack of Malboros to grab another cigarette. Wanda snatched it before she even had time to blink. “Give it b-” She lunged, but the redhead twisted away, holding it just out of reach. As if Agatha was actually a school kid.

    She threw her arms up, baffled. This was tyranny. It took her one second too long to register the way Wanda’s eyes were scanning the area - like a woman on a mission. What is she-

    “No, don’t you fucking dare-”

    But Wanda fucking dared. She threw the pack of cigarettes over Agatha’s head. They both watched as the small box flew through the air in a perfect arc - straight out of a physics book - before it disappeared into the nearest dumpster.

    Thump.

    “Yeets, as the kids say.” celebrated Wanda.

    Agatha breathed in. Breathed out. Resisted the urge to commit a felony. “Not what the kids say.” She was not about to tell Wanda Maximoff that her kids said “yeet” , as teen had regretfully taught her.

    The redhead looked pleased with herself regardless. For approximately three more seconds, that is - then, she remembered she was mad.

    Wanda put both hands on her hips again. “What’s going on with you?”

    Agatha couldn’t even begin to answer that question. Thankfully, she didn’t have to. She just spun on her heels and started walking back towards the building.

    And Wanda followed. Obviously.

    “Agatha, come back here!”

    Agatha did not come back here.

    Wanda huffed and sped up. “You called me at one AM.”

    “Oh? Did I?” She sped up her pace even more.

    “Mhm. You were asking weird questions about restricted files. You yelled, called me a psycho, then you hung up.”

    Agatha stopped, swiftly turning around and pointing at Wanda like she just had a breakthrough. “That does sound like me!”

    The redhead halted too, crossing her arms. “So I checked in with Billy.” Fucking hell.

    Agatha started walking again.

    “God, he’s such a narc,” she mumbled.

    “He says you’ve been sleeping in your office!”

    “A narc AND a liar?” She threw her hands up dramatically. “Who raised this boy?!” Agatha was almost at the glass doors that gave way to the lobby. She reached for it, but Wanda grabbed her by the elbow. She turned around, furious, and was met with the same intensity.

    “ Is he lying, Agatha?”

    Agatha hesitated. “I’m… Spending some nights in my office…" She lied. Wanda’s eyes widened, but Agatha dismissed her with a dramatic shrug. “Not necessarily sleeping . So yes, he is lying.”

    “You are spiraling,” Wanda pressed. “And now I find you sneaking around, smoking for the first time in decad- Agatha!”

    She was already inside the building.

    Hurried steps trailed right behind her, and Agatha wondered if she’d get a suspension or a simple write-up for punching a colleague right on the nose.

    “Stop running.” hissed Wanda, trying to keep her voice down as they passed through the lobby.

    Bingo.

    Agatha spun around dramatically.

    “You can’t steal people’s belongings and throw them in the trash, Maximoff!” She shouted, “You’ll be charged with theft and destruction of property!”

    Her voice echoed. Heads turned. Wanda groaned.

    “God damn it”

    They reached the elevators and Agatha started pressing the button repeatedly. Fuck what everyone says, she knows this makes the damn thing arrive faster.

    Agatha made a point not to look at Maximoff, but she could see the faint silhouette of a very pissed off redhead on the edges of her field of vision. Hands on hips. “We need to talk.”

    She didn’t answer. She did not need that right now. Agatha was doing fine! There was absolutely nothing wrong. Not anything that concerned Wanda, at least… Well, kind of. Point is: she could handle it by herself, without the help of Mrs. Fiery Housewife over there. Ding.

    The elevator doors opened.

    “Agatha. You’re acting weird.”

    “Noted. Great talk! We should do this more often.”

    She took one step into the elevator and turned around. Wanda tried to join her by stepping towards Agatha’s left. Agatha stepped to the left, blocking her path. Wanda tried to sneak through her right, Agatha stepped to the right. The redhead let out a frustrated whine. “Are you serious?”

    “You’re a terrible dancer, toots.”

    “I’m worried about you.”

    “Then… don’t be!” Agatha shrugged, grand and dramatic. “Problem solved!” The doors started closing - which may or may not have been related to the fact that Agatha had sneakily pressed the “get this shit moving right now” button.

    “You owe me eight dollars for the cigarettes,” she said with a wink.

    Nailed it. Dodged. Easy.

    Except Wanda was apparently willing to risk losing a limb today, because she jammed her arm between the doors at the last second. They bounced back open and she slid inside, smug and victorious.

    Agatha groaned. “Don’t ruin my dramatic exits, toots. You know how much they mean to me.”

    Wanda just smirked, arms crossed again, clearly waiting for privacy. The doors shut. Agatha sighed.

    “Oookay, here we go.”

    “What’s wrong with you?”

    “You want that list alphabetically or chronologically sorted?”

    “Agatha, I’m serious.”

    “Me too!” Agatha looked up with a smile, like she was recalling fond memories. “It all started when I was born. According to my mother, I was already evil and incorrigi-” “Stop.” Wanda pinched the bridge of her nose. “You’re deflecting, and you’re being a child. You’re not funny. ”

    Agatha placed a hand over her heart.

    “That’s the nicest thing you’ve ever said to me.”

    Wanda looked like she aged five years on the spot.

    “You can’t joke your way out of this. Not with me. So either talk, or I’ll start asking questions you really don’t want me to ask”

    Agatha paused.

    Fucking Billy.

    “He told you didn’t you?”

    “About a psychopath leaving you a love note? Or about you using his boyfriend to tamper with evidence?”

    She sighed. “This has nothing to do with that.”

    “I don’t believe you.”

    The elevator dinged, and both of them stepped out. Before Agatha could open the glass doors to the Behavior Analysis Unit, Wanda grabbed her arm again, stopping her in place. “You and I are having dinner tonight. No dodging, no excuses.”

    Still trying to dodge this fucking obstacle in her already very complicated path, Agatha smirked.

    “Wanda Maximoff, you’re a married woman! What will your dear husband think?” “That our old friend has gone absolutely insane and needs help.”

    Agatha tsked. “Still trying to get me alone after all these years… ”

    Wanda didn’t flinch. “Tonight. We’re gonna talk about this properly. No bullshit, no standup routine. You’re gonna eat something that isn’t takeout. At your place.” “No!” Agatha jumped up, eyes widening before she could stop herself. Wanda’s eyebrows shot up.

    “No?” She searched Agatha’s face for an answer. “You can’t go back home?” With a huff, Agatha freed herself from her grasp. She shook a wave of messy hair away from her face. “Don’t be ridiculous, of course I can.”

    “Good. Tonight then. I’ll drive you there, so you don’t run.” Wanda’s face turned into a bright smile when she spotted someone on the other side of the glass, inside the BAU. She waved, and when Agatha turned, it was Billy - looking mortified .

    Ding.

    The elevator doors opened behind Wanda, and Jen looked like she was considering turning back around upon seeing Agatha’s face.

    The redhead swiftly made her way into the now empty elevator. “Hello, Agent Kale. See you tonight, Agnes!” She waved as Jen made hesitant steps towards the unit chief. The two women stared at each other, one in anger, one in mild confusion. “You smell like an ashtray.”

    “You smell like cheap skin care,” Agatha took a deep breath, dramatically opening the doors to the BAU. She focused on Billy. “Hello, traitor!”

    Agatha was finishing one last cigarette before Wanda got out of the building. It was evening, the sky was dark, but still blue, and she was already regretting saying yes to this ridiculous dinner. In fact, she didn’t even remember saying yes.

    “Ready?” asked Wanda, chirpy with victory.

    She didn’t answer, she just threw her cigarette away and started walking. Wanda pulled her to the other side, locking their arms together like they were two old-timey housewives taking a stroll in the neighborhood.

    “I’m driving, car is over there. I’m gonna cook you something nice! What do you have in your kitchen anyway?”

    Agatha shot her a glance. “Wine.”

    “We’ll stop by a grocery store.”

    “No, no, no. I have food.”

    Let’s make this quick, for fuck’s sake.

    “Agatha, come on. You've been here for days. What do you even have left? And I know November is a hard month for you, but you gotta stop drinking.”

    Suddenly, Agatha’s whole body went cold. She stopped walking.

    “What day is it?” Her words came out strangled, barely above a whisper. Wanda blinked, confused for a second.

    “Of the month, Wanda. What day is it?”

    “It’s… not until next week.”

    Agatha swallowed dryly, forcing herself to nod. “Okay.”

    Relief and shame crashed within her like a wave against her already dodgy composure. Her knees felt weak, but Agatha finally exhaled. She hadn’t been able to ask the real question, but Wanda had answered it anyway.

    Did I miss it? Did the fucking day of my son’s death pass by me unnoticed? It never had before. Every single year, no matter what was happening in her life, Agatha counted down the days of the eleventh month. As the date crept closer, she counted the hours - almost obsessively, always watching the clock.

    She always took the week off. Let herself crumble for a while. Allowed herself to be a grieving mother instead of an FBI agent - instead of Agatha Harkness, the woman who could handle anything. When the week passed, she would patch herself back together, slip her mask back on, and pretend as if nothing had happened. No one ever questioned why she was meaner than usual. But through it all, she always had Nicky on her mind. Through and through. And it always hurt.

    Nothing - nothing - had ever made her forget.

    Until now.

    She was so angry, she suddenly felt lightheaded.

    “Are you feeling okay?” Wanda put both hands on her shoulders, but Agatha could barely feel them. Her stomach was turning inside out.

    “I’m fine,” she said, making her shaky way to the car. “Let’s just go.” The ride was quiet, and when she arrived, Herb was watering Agatha’s plants. At night. Wanda shot her a glance, but Agatha just sighed.

    “That means he wants to tell me something. Probably been doing this every evening waiting for me to come back. He’s skittish, that one.”

    “You can be scary,” Wanda shrugged. “Give me the keys, I’ll get dinner started.” Agatha hesitated. Wanda could be walking right into a trap. If Rio’s concern for her life was anything to go by, her house was still one of the most dangerous places in Quantico right now.

    “It’ll just be a second, toots. Just wait for me.”

    The two got out of the car, and Agatha made her way to the large black man watering her plants - he had his back turned to her, and he was whistling. Carefree. Agatha almost envied him right now.

    She knew next to nothing about Herb - couldn’t even remember his last name - save by the fact that he lived next door, and that he often used his hose to keep Agatha’s front yard plants watered, even though they didn’t cross over to his property. He also brought her food once or twice. “Made too much pasta”, “I have all these extra donuts”, things of that sort. She neither liked or disliked Herb, but she couldn’t help but to appreciate his presence a little bit. The way someone appreciates a well-placed stop sign in a residential street. The only time the two had exchanged more than three sentences was when Agatha spotted Dottie from the HOA trying to flag her down the street. She promptly pretended to be engaged in a really emotional conversation with a very confused Herb. To this day the man probably still remembers the day his weird stoic neighbor suddenly fell into his arms in an incoherent sob. He tried to console her, patting her on the back awkwardly and saying vague things like “It’s gonna be okay” and “there, there”. As soon as Agatha spotted Dottie making a u-turn back down the street, she jolted back up with a smile, thanked Herb and went right back to her errands run.

    Yeah, he was good to have around. Right now, though, Agatha had no time for that. She walked to the man, noticing how the faint street lights were shining on his bald head as he whistled to the tune of One Way Or Another with his back to the agent. “Herb.”

    “Jesus!” She startled him, and the hose water went up for a second. He turned around with his hand on his chest. “You can’t sneak up on me like that!”

    “You’re in my yard.”

    “Well. Your plants were dying,” he scolded with newfound bravado. “And… I wanted to talk to you.”

    “Clearly,” she said, her tone sharp as glass. Agatha nodded towards her front door. “Make it quick, I got people over.”

    Herb noticed Wanda behind her and shot the redhead a shy wave.

    “It’s just- you haven’t been around in a while…”

    “Uh-hum.”

    “And there’s some weird stuff going on around your house.”

    Agatha paused. “Weird how?”

    “First of all this man came over, rang your bell, and just stood there for a long ass time.” “Fat, white hair?”

    Herb considered it for a second. “Yeah, a bit on the heavy side with the belly. Would be a good mall Santa.”

    Fucking Konstantin.

    “Got it. Did he go in?”

    “No, I don’t think he tried. I talked to him, but he didn’t even say anything back. Not like the other-” He stopped himself.

    Agatha squinted. “Who?”

    “Look, I’m not one to judge…” he raised his hands, and Agatha had to side step the hose. “… sorry- I’ve just. Been your neighbor for a while, right? I’ve seen plenty of angry ladies banging on your door. But this one took the cake.”

    Oh, fuck no.

    “Shush.”

    Agatha immediately got concerned Wanda would overhear. Thankfully, Herb wasn’t one to ask many questions. He hunched over, as if that would hide his giant frame from the redhead.

    “What did she look like?” Agatha asked. It could be Natasha, Rio or anyone else they had sent to finish her off.

    “Latina, about five foot six, crazy eyes.”

    Rio.

    “She was pissed you wouldn’t answer. Went to town banging on your door. When I asked her what was wrong, she just hissed at me. Like a cat.”

    Definitely Rio.

    “Okay.” Agatha sighed, running a hand through her hair. “How many times did you see her?” “Three times. Twice at night. Banging and yelling. Something about... giving her proof of life?”

    She couldn’t help but huff out a laugh, but motioned for Herb to continue. “She tried to break in, but I chased her away with the hose.” He illustrated by pressing down on the tip of it to make the stream go stronger. “I tried calling your work but it’s hell to get through to anyone in there. A lot of paperwork.”

    “Tell me about it,” she mumbled. “Thanks, man. For the. Hose.”

    “Yeah no worries. ” He looked proud. “Oh, and one last thing…” She waited - Herb seemed to be trying to find the right words. “I didn’t want to pry, so I left it alone… don’t be offended or anything but…”

    “Spit it out.”

    “There’s a nasty smell coming from your porch.”

    Agatha turned around immediately.

    Apparently, Wanda had noticed the same thing. She was crouching, arms reaching for something nestled deep inside one of the many shrubs that circled Agatha’s house. The redhead moved hesitantly, as if she already knew, on some subconscious level, that whatever she was about to touch would be wrong.

    Agatha’s annoyance with Herb disappeared in a second, alarm bells ringing in her mind. “Wanda?” She said loudly, trying to sound casual, but the caution in her voice was unmistakable. “What are you doing, toots?”

    Wanda stood as she carefully pulled the object free. The fabric was dirty, clearly beaten up by the November frost, but its deep purple colour was still familiar. It was Agatha’s missing scarf. Wanda’s face instinctively flinched away from it, nose scrunching up in disgust. Soon after, Agatha felt it too. A foul odour.

    “Oh, yeah, that’s the smell-” Herb started, flinching as well. Agatha’s head snapped towards the man.

    “Leave. Now.”

    He didn’t need to be told twice.

    “Sorry. I’m-” Herb nonsensically apologized, stumbling his way out of Agatha’s garden. She turned back to Wanda, who had turned pale.

    The bundle sagged in Wanda’s hands. For a moment, it almost looked like she was holding a newborn baby. Weighted. Limp. But something was off - the shape beneath the cloth seemed to be changing, sluggishly shifting under Wanda’s fingers.

    Agatha’s eyes widened.

    The irony was almost poetic - how Agatha had failed to recognize the smell at first. She had been around so many dead bodies in her life. Yet still couldn’t place the putrid odor of death when it was coming from her own house.

    “Wanda…” Her hand rose up in a warning as she stepped forward.

    But Wanda didn’t seem to hear her. She was frowning, her grip adjusting the bundle, head shaking slightly, trying to understand-

    And then, it moved.

    From inside.

    A sickening, rippling pulse went through the object, like something alive was squirming beneath the fabric.

    Wanda screeched. Her hands jerked away immediately, arms frantically flapping in the air. The bundle dropped to the grass with a heavy thud. It rolled slightly, making the cloth unfold.

    Agatha didn’t need to get any closer to understand, but she stepped forward anyway. Hundreds of maggots spilled over the ground - a squirming mass writhing over the black remains of something long past dead. They seemed to be multiplying, tiny glistening bodies were erupting from the center and falling down the sides, flickering uselessly once they hit the grass. Agatha was hit with a warm putrid gush of air that smothered her senses and filled the air around them. She covered her nose with the back of her hand.

    “What the hell is this?!” Wanda choked out, voice trembling.

    “Was,” Agatha corrected without thinking.

    Her brain-to-mouth filter was gone for the moment, her mind was too distracted with images of days ago, when she had opened her door to find Rio on her porch. The girl had been startled by Agatha’s arrival, tossing something into that very same shrub. Agatha searched her brain for something - anything. And then, it hit her.

    Oh, Rio... What did you do?

    It looked and smelled exactly like one would expect a days old corpse left in the cold November weather. The decay had been slowed down by the temperature, and the shrub protected it against scavengers, but there was no stopping death’s process. The dog had no chance.

    Wanda, of course, had no such clarity. The redhead spun around, walking towards the shrubs and crouching again. Agatha almost sighed in relief. It was too rotten for Wanda to recognize. She was examining the crime scene.

    Phew.

    But Wanda returned to the rotten carcass. She was holding a wooden stick she had taken from the floor. Agatha sighed.

    “Wanda, honey, let’s just go back inside,” she tried, to no avail.

    “There’s-” she was panting, clearly nervous.

    “It’s just a cat or something…” Terrible attempt, but Agatha was committed now. “...someone probably abandoned it here.”

    “There’s something shining.”

    Not from where Agatha was standing there wasn’t. But Wanda’s eyes were locked on the carcass. Fuck. Why won’t she look away? That thing was gross.

    “I think…”

    “Wait,” Agatha tried to buy herself time, her own heart now quickening, pulse thumping against her ears. “Let me see.”

    She slowly moved around the body. When she reached Wanda’s side, she saw it. A piece of metal was reflecting the light coming from Agatha’s porch. It was attached to a blue dog collar Wanda must’ve identified just as quickly as Agatha had recognized her own scarf. She looked at the redhead.

    Shit.

    Agatha knew that look. She had seen it on her own face when thinking about Rio in front of the bathroom mirror. A look that says ‘I know the truth, but I’ll hold on to a comforting lie for as long as I can.’

    Shit, shit, shit.

    Wanda leaned forward, stick in hand.

    “Don’t-” Agatha tried, softly gripping Wanda’s shoulder.

    Too late. With the other side of the stick, Wanda started to spread the vermin away as best as she could, revealing the partial rotten skeletons of a small animal. It was clearly too big to be a cat.

    “Leave it alone, hon.” She tried pulling Wanda’s elbow, but the woman dodged Agatha’s touch like it burned.

    Suddenly, the thin wooden branch snapped in half as Wanda shoved it forcefully on the side of the carcass. The strength of it had been enough to make the body turn around, its weight crushing the maggots underneath it. A repulsive wet sound made Agatha’s face contort - somehow, the smell managed to get even worse.

    Wanda had accomplished her mission. The dirty metal was now fully facing the two women, shining under the faint light behind them. It was a name plaque, of course. In the back, you’d find Wanda’s cell phone number. Agatha winced at that thought, knowing Rio’s phone most definitely had a contact named “Agent Toots” saved by now. The engraving in the front was no surprise to Agatha.

    Sparky Agatha squished her eyes closed, waiting for Wanda to scream again.

    Nothing.

    She glanced at the woman beside her. Her face was stoic, but Agatha knew better - she could almost see the cogs turning inside Wanda’s head. Her mind was surely at a million miles per hour. Green eyes were glued to the shiny name plaque - a confirmation of what both of them already knew.

    “Sparky?!” Agatha feigned surprise. “What the fuck! Is this your Sparky?!” Wanda didn’t answer.

    That stupid fucking dog. How was Agatha ever going to explain this? She barely understood it herself. It was due to jealousy, of course. But why would Rio fucking throw it in her doorstep? When did she even fucking do it? Right after leaving Agatha’s place…

    We fought about Wanda that day.

    It wasn’t about that. It was about the meeting. Wanda cancelled it to look for Sparky. Then we went on a date.

    Rio scrambled to find something to do. She wasn’t expecting a date.

    She was expecting a text, though. Maybe a call. Probably got angry I didn’t find her number on the files.

    And then killed Wanda’s fucking dog? That’s psycho.

    Well no SHIT.

    “Did…” Wanda started, snapping Agatha’s attention back. “Did you do this?” Agatha couldn’t help a loud scoff. “Are you fucking kidding me?”

    Wanda crossed her arms. “I don’t know! You’ve been acting crazy lately!” “Wanda!”

    The redhead started pacing around. “You’ve been smoking, calling me at one AM, sleeping in the office... maybe-” She stopped and sighed, arms dropping in surrender. “Maybe you’re feeling guilty! I don’t know.”

    Agatha was speechless. Wanda shot her a tired shrug of defeat - as if she didn’t have the energy to think of any other explanation. Suddenly, Agatha felt angry. “Yeah, Wanda. Why not! Cigarettes are a classic gateway drug to murdering your friend’s dog - everyone knows that.”

    They stared at each other in silence for a second. The only sounds were both of their breaths and the writhing from the corpse beneath them. Wanda didn’t seem convinced. Agatha scoffed.

    “Jesus Christ, I know I’m shady, but I didn’t realize I was that shady.” “You’re-”

    “I’m many things, Maximoff!” She threw her hands up. “But I’m not a dog killer!” That was a lie.

    She’d had a fucked up childhood, okay? Agatha had chosen to play the part her mom had set up for her. Up until she was eighteen, she’d run around the city setting abandoned buildings on fire, stealing anything she could get her hands on, and fighting anyone who even looked at her the wrong way. She’d sell fake drugs to high school and college kids - and real drugs once in a while, if her client was older and not to be fucked with. And yes, she had killed a dog once. It was a warning. An annoying dog from a classmate who threatened to expose her little business ventures.

    But Wanda didn’t know that.

    The redhead sighed, pinching her temples. “Okay. Fine. You didn’t kill my dog.” “Glad we settled that.”

    “Who did?”

    Agatha threw her arms up again and huffed through flimsy lips - as though completely dumbstruck by the question. “Beats me!”

    Wanda tilted her head and squinted - telling Agatha she wasn’t buying it. “Really! How the hell would I know, toots?”

    “It’s in your fucking house, Agatha. And don’t toots me.”

    “I didn’t know it was here!”

    “Was it her? The Green Witch? The one sending you little love notes?” Don’t say it.

    “...Probably.”

    Fuck. Play it cool.

    “What? She’s been here? Watching you?”

    Agatha shrugged, cocky and theatrical. She shot Wanda a smile, trying to lighten the mood. “Can you blame her?”

    It didn’t work.

    “If you don’t tell me what’s going on right now, I’m going to call the fucking bureau and demand they take you to a safe house and make you go through mental health evaluation.” Oof. Bitch move.

    Agatha felt the weight of psychiatric evaluation paperwork immediately drop on top of her - like an anvil crushing a cartoon character. She’d be withdrawn from duty for months. No gun, no badge, no access to any of the FBI’s databases. Even if she didn’t end up in a safe house, she’d have cameras around her house, babysitter bodyguards and she’d have to go to therapy, for fuck’s sake. Obliged to fill up “how are you feeling” forms every other day. She’d have to journal.

    Agatha shuddered at the thought.

    Someone in Wanda’s position could definitely pull that shit off. Force Agatha into her own personal bureaucratic hell. Everyone trusted Wanda - and not a lot of people liked Agatha. Plus, people already thought Agatha needed therapy.

    “You wouldn’t do that to me,” Agatha tried to appeal to friendship. The offense in her tone wasn’t even feigned. She was a psychologist! She should be threatening other people with obligatory journaling, not the other way around.

    “Try me.”

    They stood in silence for a second.

    Agatha sighed. “Fucking hell, Maximoff... alright.”

    Wanda waited, eyebrows raised and hands on her hips. Agatha pitied Teen and Tommy - maybe even Vision - for having to deal with that daily. She had no idea how to dodge the furious redhead.

    “Start making sense.” ordered Wanda.

    “She was around. It was likely her.”

    “Why did she kill my dog?”

    Agatha hesitated. “...Jealousy?”

    Wanda’s face dropped. “What? How does she know- Have you been in contact with her?” “Yes. No. A little.”

    “Agatha!”

    “We had… a couple of meetups.”

    “In person?!”

    Stop talking immediately.

    Yeah, I have to shut the fuck up.

    Unfortunately, Agatha’s mouth worked quicker. “I didn’t know it was her! She didn’t come up at the bar and introduced-”

    “Oh my god!” Wanda cut her off. “Did you sleep with her?!”

    What did I just say?

    Agatha was too busy chastising herself to answer immediately. Wanda caught up on it, because of course she did. The redhead sunk her forehead into her wrists - no doubt still squeamish from touching that dead dog with her bare fingers.

    “Oh my god, Agatha…”

    “It was one time.” She paused. “Two times.”

    I swear to fucking god.

    Wanda’s head snapped towards her. Agatha knew she should stop, but what was the point now? She was resigned, and she couldn’t take another fucking night at that goddamn office sofa.

    “Two times?”

    “Three, but it was kind of a weird one. We got thrown out of a restaurant-” “I cannot fucking believe you!”

    “I did not know!”

    “I don’t believe that either! No way you didn’t see it. You’re too good for that.” And there it was. Agatha recoiled, physically taking a step back. Wanda was furious - and rightfully so. Agatha did see it, and both of them knew it. She had put all of them in danger like an idiot.

    Ding.

    Agatha’s phone chimed in her pocket. She froze, recognizing the distinct sound of another fucking text message.

    Saved by the bell?

    Another chime.

    Don’t look at it.

    But once again, she couldn’t help herself. Agatha reached for her pocket and slid her phone out just enough to see the screen. She casually unlocked it, as if she wasn’t negotiating her whole career right and possibly her life right at this moment. The screen flashed with a message. Then a bunch more, in quick succession.

    Unknown: Why is she there?

    Unknown: You need some work-life balance, you know?

    Unknown: 🐶 🪦 Unknown: I can explain that, btw Unknown: Just glad your alive x Wrong “you’re”. Asshole.

    She quickly shoved her phone back into her pocket, trying to keep her expression neutral and fighting the urge to look around. Could Rio hear her through the fucking phone? Agatha wouldn’t be surprised at that point.

    “Was that her?” asked Wanda, her voice dripping with sarcasm.

    Wanda was joking, but she was spot on. Yes, it was her.

    “No, it wasn’t her.” Agatha lied. “It’s… my girlfriend.”

    Oh my god.

    What is wrong with me today?

    “The one I mentioned on the phone,” Agatha quickly added, trying to fix it. Thankfully, Wanda didn’t seem surprised Agatha would cheat on a girlfriend with a serial killer. She was still good at this - Rio did not take away her ability to make shit up on the spot. “It’s not The Green Witch.”

    “What’s her name?”

    Agatha paused, genuinely confused. “My girlfriend’s?”

    “No, you idiot!” Wanda lashed out. “The fucking serial killer trying to murder you!” “Natalie,” she blurted out before she could think twice. “Natalie Rushmore. It was probably a fake name. Redhead. I have a type.”

    “No you fucking don’t.” she mumbled between a sigh. Then, her eyes widened. “She got Sparky from my house. She knows where I live! With my family, Agatha! How could you fucking keep this a secret?”

    “It’s over, okay! She’s not even in Quantico anymore! I drove her away!” “Where is she?”

    Agatha scoffed. “If I knew, she’d be in handcuffs.”

    “Fuzzy ones, apparently,” Wanda mumbled, looking away to gather her thoughts. When she looked back at Agatha, she had resolve written all over her face.

    “Find her.”

    “I’m trying, toots.”

    “No, you’re not. Call Penelope.”

    Fucking hell, why didn’t she tell me this before?

    “Garcia is back?!”

    She could’ve gone right through those restricted files. If there was anyone more powerful than the entire FBI IT department, it was hacker-turned-federal-agent Penelope Garcia. And she loved Agatha. It would’ve been a breeze.

    “Yes. And Tommy comes back home on Wednesday. You have until then to arrest The Green Witch.”

    Agatha couldn’t believe it. “What? It’s fucking Friday!”

    “Don’t care. On Wednesday, I’m calling this in.”

    “Tell Toby to get a hotel room!”

    “Tommy.”

    She dismissed it with a wave. “That’s what I said. Come on.”

    “No. I can’t live like this. And I will not have my kids murdered by your one night stand.” She scoffed. “Two-night stand.”

    Wanda shot her a glance.

    “Okay, sorry!” Agatha shot her hands up in surrender. “This is just unrealistic! She could be anywhere!”

    That was another lie. Well, not really. Natasha could be anywhere. Rio was definitely around.

    “Not my problem.”

    “Wanda, come fucking on. You gave me four fucking days to conclude a federal investigation!”

    Agatha’s gaze was fierce - searching for any mercy - her blue eyes were an unstoppable force, but Wanda’s green ones were an immovable object. The redhead leaned forward, whispering with as much rage as Agatha felt.

    “Well, Agnes… I guess you best hurry up, then .”

    She only had until Wednesday, but the doorbell still rang later that same night. Agatha had just showered after managing to get rid of the corpse rotting in her front yard, and now she was going to get yelled at for it for the second time. It wasn’t like shouting was going to bring the fucking dog back to life, but apparently Wanda couldn’t even get home before terrorizing her again. Fantastic.

    Groaning, Agatha peeled herself off the couch, swung the door open without a second thought and-

    Froze.

    “Did you know that it is a universally acknowledged truth that a lady cop cannot be good at her job and have a healthy personal life at the same time?”

    That. What? That’s not-

    Wide brown eyes watched her with expectation. A familiar smirk, a mischievous posture - both hands behind her back, like a magician about to reveal a trick. She looked very pleased with herself.

    Not Wanda.

    Rio Vidal stood on her porch, her grin widening as she pulled a large pizza box from behind her.

    “Hungry?”
